# GPN list — the only queue

**Every item has exactly one of four states.** No others exist.

| state | meaning |
|---|---|
| `NEW` | added, not yet in a version |
| `IN-PROGRESS` | bundled into a version that is being built/released |
| `COMPLETE` | shipped: version published, checklist fully ticked, node reports it |
| `BLOCKED` | cannot be completed now — reason recorded, and what would unblock it |

## The loop

1. Items are added as `NEW`.
2. Starting a version: **bundle every `NEW` item**, mark them `IN-PROGRESS`.
3. `scripts/make-release-checklist.py <version>` copies the generic checklist and inserts every
   `IN-PROGRESS` item at the right place.
4. The cron works that checklist in order, ticking each box with evidence.
5. When every box is ticked **and** the version is published **and** a node reports it from its
   running process, the bundled items become `COMPLETE`.
6. Bundle the next batch of `NEW` items.

A `BLOCKED` item stays on the list with its reason; its checklist line is ticked
`blocked — <reason>` so it never holds a release hostage.

Nothing is ever deleted. `COMPLETE` and `BLOCKED` rows are the record.
Migrated from GPN-TASKS.md on 2026-11-17 — that file remains the archive, this one is the queue.

Mechanism (read, not inferred): `gpn-node/node/src/sync_tolerance.rs:141` resets a peer's abort count when `elapsed >= backoff × count`, and `DISCONNECTED_PEER_BACKOFF = 60 s` with count stuck at 1 ⇒ the entry expires after 60 s. At this cadence the count therefore cannot reach the SDK's 3 (it only rises when two aborts land inside `60 s × count`, as that one pair shows), and the module's own comment at `:76` — *“a peer that abandons this many requests is still throttled by the SDK's own backoff (60 s × count), which we leave in place”* — does not hold for a 70 s retry cadence: the throttle degenerates to a constant 60 s that the peer always outwaits. Second effect, and it is measured rather than projected: `INFLIGHT_PENALTY_BUDGET = 1<<16` drains at −1024 per abort ⇒ 64 aborts exhaust it. Net: item F removed the SDK's terminal action (ban at 3) for this condition without supplying a replacement terminal action, so a peer that never makes progress is now served a fresh substream every 70 s forever. | 190 | COMPLETE | `scripts/release-status.sh` reports "IN FLIGHT" for a HEAD that simply is not on origin — it has now hidden rung 1 from three consecutive maintainer ticks — measured 2026-11-15 20:20Z: script prints `tree v0.3.47 @ 0cb9d7e` / `site 0.3.47` / `ci none` / `builds none` / `lock absent` and exits 1 with `in flight: CI none`, while the GitHub API says the tip `origin/main` `499633e` is `completed/success` (run 43746262474) and the last six runs are all success. Root cause: `ci` is resolved by matching `head_sha=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)` against runs, so a local HEAD that was never pushed yields the literal string `none`, which falls into the catch-all `*)` arm and is treated as "pending" ⇒ exit 1. It also names only the local sha, so the reader cannot tell "unpushed local divergence" from "release in flight". Second-order cost: rung 1 is the first gate of the maintainer ladder, so every tick on a diverged checkout starts by believing a release is building. Fix direction: `ci none` ⇒ `OK/idle` (rc unchanged) with an explicit line `local HEAD <sha> has no CI run (not on origin); origin/main <sha> is <status>`; keep `queued`/`in_progress` as the only in-flight CI strings, and treat a *failed to read the API* as unknown (never as success). Add a self-test with a stubbed `git rev-parse`/API payload for: success, in_progress, none-with-origin-green, none-with-origin-red, API unreachable. | 2026-11-15 12:2x |

| 198 | COMPLETE | P0 — the node must run with NO visible console. The `GPN-Node` task runs `cmd /c start-node.cmd --task` as an interactive logon task, so the node's stdout appears in a console the user can see, and closing it kills the node (`console-closed`, exit -1073741510). Launch the worker through `wscript hidden-launch.vbs` (WScript.Shell.Run window style 0), keep stdout going to `node.log`, and leave `start-gpn.cmd` as the only visible window (the startup summary, safe to close). Original notes: done when: the panel states source + window; the number equals chain-observed rewards for that window within 1%; a restart mid-window neither resets nor double-counts; and a disagreement between ledger and chain renders as a visible mismatch, not a wrong number.|
